p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}) = \frac {1}{\left(2 \pi \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t}}\right) ^ {d / 2}} \exp \left(- \frac {\alpha_ {t} \left\| x _ {t - 1} - \mu_ {t} ^ {\star} (x _ {t}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (1 - \alpha_ {t})} + \zeta_ {t} (x _ {t - 1}, x _ {t})\right) \tag {81}
$$

holds for some residual term  $\zeta_t(x_{t-1}, x_t)$  obeying

$$
\left| \zeta_ {t} \left(x _ {t - 1}, x _ {t}\right) \right| \leq c _ {\zeta} d ^ {2} \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) \log^ {2} T. \tag {82}
$$

Here, we recall the definition of  $\mu_t^\star (x_t)$  in (74b).

By comparing Lemma 8 with expression (76a), we see that when restricted to the set  $\mathcal{E}$ , the two conditional distributions  $p_{X_{t-1} \mid X_t}(x_{t-1} \mid x_t)$  and  $p_{Y_{t-1} \mid Y_t}(x_{t-1} \mid x_t)$  (i.e., informally, the time-reversed transition kernels) are fairly close to each other, a crucial observation that suggests the validity of the diffusion generative model.

Furthermore, we are also in need of bounding the ratio of the two conditional distributions when going beyond the set  $\mathcal{E}$ . As it turns out, it suffices to develop a crude bound on the logarithm of such ratios (which are used in defining the KL divergence), as stated in the following lemma.

Lemma 9. For all  $(x_{t},x_{t - 1})\in \mathbb{R}^{d}\times \mathbb{R}^{d}$ , it holds that

$$
\log \frac {p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}\right)}{p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {*} \mid Y _ {t}} \left(x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}\right)} \leq 2 T \left(\| x _ {t - 1} - \widehat {x} _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \| x _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + T ^ {2 c _ {R}}\right). \tag {83}
$$

The proof of Lemma 9 is provided in Appendix C.2.

Step 3: bounding the KL divergence between  $p_{X_{t-1}}|_{X_t}$  and  $p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}}|_{Y_t}$ . Equipped with the above two lemmas, we are positioned to first control the KL divergence between  $p_{X_{t-1}}|_{X_t}$  and  $p_{Y_{t-1}^{\star}}|_{Y_t}$ ; by doing so we are neglecting the impact of the score estimation error for the moment (see the definition (75) of  $Y_{t-1}^{\star}$ ). It is first seen from Lemma 8 and (76a) that: for any  $(x_t, x_{t-1}) \in \mathcal{E}$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t})}{p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {*}} | Y _ {t} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t})} = \exp \left(O \left(d ^ {2} \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) \log^ {2} T\right)\right) = 1 + O \left(d ^ {2} \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) \log^ {2} T\right) \\ = 1 + O \left(d ^ {2} \frac {\log^ {3} T}{T}\right) \in \left[ \frac {1}{2}, 2 \right], \tag {84} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last line results from (39b) and the assumption that  $T \geq c_{10}d^{2}\log^{3}T$  for some large enough constant  $c_{10} > 0$ . We can then calculate

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {x _ {t} \sim q _ {t}} \left[ \mathrm {K L} \left(p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (\cdot \mid x _ {t}) \| p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {\star} \mid Y _ {t}} (\cdot \mid x _ {t})\right) \right]
$$

$$
= \Big (\int_ {\mathcal {E}} + \int_ {\mathcal {E} ^ {\mathrm {c}}} \Big) p _ {X _ {t}} (x _ {t}) p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}) \log \frac {p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t})}{p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {*} \mid Y _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t})} \mathrm {d} x _ {t - 1} \mathrm {d} x _ {t},
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {\text {(i)}} {=} \int_ {\mathcal {E}} p _ {X _ {t}} (x _ {t}) \left\{p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}\right) - p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {*} \mid Y _ {t}} \left(x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}\right) \right. \\ \left. + p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}\right) \cdot O \left(\left(\frac {p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {*} \mid Y _ {t}} \left(x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}\right)}{p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}\right)} - 1\right) ^ {2}\right) \right\} d x _ {t - 1} d x _ {t} \\ + \int_ {\mathcal {E} ^ {\mathrm {c}}} p _ {X _ {t}} (x _ {t}) p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}) \log \frac {p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t})}{p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {\star} \mid Y _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t})} \mathrm {d} x _ {t - 1} \mathrm {d} x _ {t} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {\text {(i i)}} {=} \int_ {\mathcal {E}} p _ {X _ {t}} (x _ {t}) \left\{p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}) - p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {*} \mid Y _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}) + p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}) O \left(d ^ {4} \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) ^ {2} \log^ {4} T\right) \right\} d x _ {t - 1} d x _ {t} \\ + \int_ {\mathcal {E} ^ {c}} p _ {X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t}\right) p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}\right) \left\{2 T \left(\| x _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \| x _ {t - 1} - \widehat {x} _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + T ^ {2 c _ {R}}\right) \right\} \mathrm {d} x _ {t - 1} \mathrm {d} x _ {t}. \tag {85} \\ \end{array}
$$

Here, (i) results from the elementary fact that: if  $\left|\frac{p_Y(x)}{p_X(x)} - 1\right| < \frac{1}{2}$ , then the Taylor expansion gives

$$
p _ {X} (x) \log \frac {p _ {X} (x)}{p _ {Y} (x)} = - p _ {X} (x) \log \left(1 + \frac {p _ {Y} (x) - p _ {X} (x)}{p _ {X} (x)}\right)
$$

$$
= p _ {X} (x) - p _ {Y} (x) + p _ {X} (x) O \left(\left(\frac {p _ {Y} (x)}{p _ {X} (x)} - 1\right) ^ {2}\right);
$$

regarding (ii), we invoke (84) and Lemma 9.

To continue, let us bound each term on the right-hand side of (85) separately. From the definition of the set  $\mathcal{E}$  (cf. (80)), direct calculations yield

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \big ((X _ {t}, X _ {t - 1}) \notin \mathcal {E} \big) = \int_ {(x _ {t}, x _ {t - 1}) \notin \mathcal {E}} p _ {X _ {t} - 1} (x _ {t - 1}) p _ {X _ {t} | X _ {t - 1}} (x _ {t} | x _ {t - 1}) \mathrm {d} x _ {t - 1} \mathrm {d} x _ {t} \\ = \int_ {\left(x _ {t}, x _ {t - 1}\right) \notin \mathcal {E}} p _ {X _ {t} - 1} \left(x _ {t - 1}\right) \frac {1}{\left(2 \pi \left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right)\right) ^ {d / 2}} \exp \left(- \frac {\left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\alpha_ {t}} x _ {t - 1} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right)}\right) d x _ {t - 1} d x _ {t} \\ \leq \exp (- c _ {3} d \log T), \tag {86} \\ \end{array}
$$

and similarly,

$$
\int_ {\left(x _ {t - 1}, x _ {t}\right) \notin \mathcal {E}} p _ {X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t}\right) p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}\right) \left(2 T \left(\left\| x _ {t} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + \left\| x _ {t - 1} - \widehat {x} _ {t} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}\right) + T ^ {2 c _ {R}}\right) \mathrm {d} x _ {t - 1} \mathrm {d} x _ {t} \leq \exp \left(- c _ {3} d \log T\right). \tag {87}
$$

In addition, for every  $(x_{t},x_{t - 1})$  obeying  $\| x_{t - 1} - x_t / \sqrt{\alpha_t}\| _2 > c_3\sqrt{d(1 - \alpha_t)\log T}$  and  $-\log p_{X_t}(x_t)\leq \frac{1}{2} c_6d\log T$ , one can use the definition (74b) of  $\mu_t^\star (\cdot)$  to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| x _ {t - 1} - \mu_ {t} ^ {\star} \left(x _ {t}\right) \right\| _ {2} = \left\| x _ {t - 1} - \frac {1}{\sqrt {\alpha_ {t}}} x _ {t} - \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\sqrt {\alpha_ {t}} \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right)} \mathbb {E} \left[ x _ {t} - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} X _ {0} \mid X _ {t} = x _ {t} \right] \right\| _ {2} (88) \\ \geq \left\| x _ {t - 1} - \frac {1}{\sqrt {\alpha_ {t}}} x _ {t} \right\| _ {2} - \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\sqrt {\alpha_ {t}} (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} \mathbb {E} \Big [ \big \| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} X _ {0} \big \| _ {2} \mid X _ {t} = x _ {t} \Big ] \\ \geq c _ {3} \sqrt {d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \log T} - 6 \bar {c} _ {5} \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\sqrt {\alpha_ {t} (1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t})}} \sqrt {d \log T} \\ = \left(c _ {3} - 6 \bar {c} _ {5} \frac {\sqrt {1 - \alpha_ {t}}}{\sqrt {\alpha_ {t} (1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t})}}\right) \sqrt {d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \log T} \geq \frac {c _ {3}}{2} \sqrt {d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \log T}, (89) \\ \end{array}
$$

where the third line results from (43a) in Lemma 1, and the last line applies (39) and holds true as long as  $c_3$  is large enough. In turn, this combined with (76a) indicates that: for any  $x_t$  obeying  $-\log p_{X_t}(x_t) \leq \frac{1}{2} c_6 d \log T$ ,

$$
\int_ {x _ {t - 1}: \left\| x _ {t - 1} - x _ {t} / \sqrt {\alpha_ {t}} \right\| _ {2} > c _ {3} \sqrt {d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \log T}} p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {\star}} | Y _ {t} \left(x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}\right) \mathrm {d} x _ {t - 1} \leq \exp \left(- \frac {c _ {3}}{2} d \log T\right). \tag {90}
$$

As a result, (86) and (90) taken collectively demonstrate that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \int_ {\mathcal {E}} p _ {X _ {t}} (x _ {t}) \left\{p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}) - p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {*} \mid Y _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}) \right\} \mathrm {d} x _ {t - 1} \mathrm {d} x _ {t} \right| \\ = \left| 1 - \mathbb {P} \big ((X _ {t}, X _ {t - 1}) \notin \mathcal {E} \big) - \int_ {(x _ {t}, x _ {t - 1}) \notin \mathcal {E}} p _ {X _ {t}} (x _ {t}) \Big \{1 - p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {\star} \mid Y _ {t}} (x _ {t - 1} \mid x _ {t}) \Big \} \mathrm {d} x _ {t - 1} \mathrm {d} x _ {t} \right| \\ \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {c _ {3}}{2} d \log T\right). \tag {91} \\ \end{array}
$$

Substituting (87) and (91) into (85) yields: for each  $t \geq 2$ ,

$$
\underset {x _ {t} \sim q _ {t}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathrm {K L} \left(p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (\cdot \mid x _ {t}) \| p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {\star} \mid Y _ {t}} (\cdot \mid x _ {t})\right) \right] \lesssim d ^ {4} \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}}\right) ^ {2} \log^ {4} T + 3 \exp \left(- \frac {c _ {3}}{2} d \log T\right) \lesssim \frac {d ^ {4} \log^ {6} T}{T ^ {2}}, \tag {92}
$$

where the last inequality utilizes the properties (39) of the learning rates.

Step 4: quantifying the effect of score estimation errors. Thus far, we have quantified the KL divergence between  $p_{X_{t-1}}|_{X_t}$  and  $p_{Y_{t-1}^*}|_{Y_t}$ . Given that  $Y_{t-1}^*$  is obtained from  $Y_t$  using the true score function, we still need to control the effect of the score estimation error, which is accomplished by means of the following lemma. The proof of this lemma can be found in Appendix C.3.

Lemma 10. For any  $1 < t \leq T$ , one has

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\left[\mathsf{KL}\Big(p_{X_{t - 1}\mid X_{t}}(\cdot \mid x_{t})\parallel p_{Y_{t - 1}\mid Y_{t}}(\cdot \mid x_{t})\Big)\right] - \mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\left[\mathsf{KL}\Big(p_{X_{t - 1}\mid X_{t}}(\cdot \mid x_{t})\parallel p_{Y_{t - 1}^{*}\mid Y_{t}}(\cdot \mid x_{t})\Big)\right] \\ \lesssim \exp (- c _ {2 0} d \log T) + \frac {d \log^ {3} T}{T} \underset {X _ {t} \sim q _ {t}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (X _ {t}) ^ {2} \right] \tag {93} \\ \end{array}
$$

for some universal constant  $c_{20} > 0$ .

Step 5: putting all this together. To finish up, substitute (92), (179) and (179) into the decomposition (79) to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathsf {K L} \left(p _ {X _ {1}} \| p _ {Y _ {1}}\right) \lesssim \mathsf {K L} \left(p _ {X _ {T}} \| p _ {Y _ {T}}\right) + \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T - 1} \underset {x _ {t} \sim q _ {t}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathsf {K L} \left(p _ {X _ {t - 1} | X _ {t}} (\cdot | x _ {t}) \| p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {*} | Y _ {t}} (\cdot | x _ {t})\right) \right] \\ \left. \right. + \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {T - 1} \left\{\underset {x _ {t} \sim q _ {t}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathsf {K L} \left(p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (\cdot \mid x _ {t}) \| p _ {Y _ {t - 1} \mid Y _ {t}} (\cdot \mid x _ {t})\right)\right] - \underset {x _ {t} \sim q _ {t}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathsf {K L} \left(p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {t}} (\cdot \mid x _ {t}) \| p _ {Y _ {t - 1} ^ {\star} \mid Y _ {t}} (\cdot \mid x _ {t})\right)\right]\right\} \\ \lesssim \mathsf {K L} (p _ {X _ {T}} \| p _ {Y _ {T}}) + \sum_ {2 \leq t \leq T} \frac {d ^ {4} \log^ {6} T}{T ^ {2}} + \left(d \log^ {3} T\right) \sum_ {t = 2} ^ {T} \underset {X _ {t} \sim q _ {t}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (X _ {t}) ^ {2} \right] \\ \asymp \frac {d ^ {4} \log^ {6} T}{T} + d \varepsilon_ {\mathrm {s c o r e}} ^ {2} \log^ {3} T, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last relation applies the bound on  $\mathsf{KL}(p_{X_T} \parallel p_{Y_T})$  as in (122). This establishes Theorem 3.

# 6 Discussion

In this paper, we have developed a new suite of non-asymptotic theory for establishing the convergence and faithfulness of diffusion generative modeling, assuming access to reliable estimates of the (Stein) score functions. Our analysis framework seeks to track the dynamics of the reverse process directly using elementary tools, which eliminates the need to look at the continuous-time limit and invoke the SDE and ODE toolboxes. Only very minimal assumptions on the target data distribution are imposed. In addition to demonstrating the non-asymptotic iteration complexities of two mainstream discrete-time samplers — a deterministic sampler based on the probability flow ODE, and a DDPM-type stochastic sampler — we have discovered potential strategies to further accelerate the sampling processes, taking advantage of estimates of a small number of additional objects. The analysis framework laid out in the current paper might shed light on how to analyze other variants of score-based generative models as well.

Moving forward, there are plenty of questions that require in-depth theoretical understanding. For instance, the dimension dependency in our convergence results remains sub-optimal; can we further refine our theory in order to reveal tight dependency in this regard? To what extent can we further accelerate the sampling process, without requiring much more information than the score functions? Ideally, one would hope to achieve acceleration with the aid of the score functions only. It would also be of paramount interest to establish end-to-end performance guarantees that take into account both the score learning phase and the sampling phase.

# A Proof for several preliminary facts

# A.1 Proof of properties (38)

Elementary calculations reveal that: the  $(i,j)$ -th entry of  $J_{t}(x)$  is given by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left[ J _ {t} (x) \right] _ {i, j} = \mathbb {1} \{i = j \} + \frac {1}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \Bigg \{\Big (\int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) \big (x _ {i} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0, i} \big) \mathrm {d} x _ {0} \Big) \Big (\int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) \big (x _ {j} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0, j} \big) \mathrm {d} x _ {0} \Big) \\ \left. - \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x\right) \left(x _ {i} - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0, i}\right) \left(x _ {j} - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0, j}\right) \mathrm {d} x _ {0} \right\}. \tag {94} \\ \end{array}
$$

This immediately establishes the matrix expression (38).

# A.2 Proof of properties (39) regarding the learning rates

Proof of property (39a). From the choice of  $\beta_{t}$  in (22), we have

$$
\alpha_ {t} = 1 - \beta_ {t} \geq 1 - \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T} \geq \frac {1}{2}, \quad t \geq 2.
$$

The case with  $t = 1$  holds trivially since  $\beta_{1} = 1 / T^{c_{0}}$  for some large enough constant  $c_{0} > 0$ .

Proof of properties (39b) and (39c). We start by proving (39b). Let  $\tau$  be an integer obeying

$$
\beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {\tau} \leq 1 <   \beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {\tau + 1}, \tag {95}
$$

and we divide into two cases based on  $\tau$ .

- Consider any  $t$  satisfying  $t \leq \tau$ . In this case, it suffices to prove that

$$
1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t - 1} \geq \frac {1}{3} \beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {t}. \tag {96}
$$

Clearly, if (96) is valid, then any  $t \leq \tau$  obeys

$$
\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} = \frac {\beta_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} \leq \frac {\frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T} \beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {t}}{\frac {1}{3} \beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {t}} = \frac {3 c _ {1} \log T}{T}
$$

as claimed. Towards proving (96), first note that the base case with  $t = 2$  holds true trivially since  $1 - \overline{\alpha}_1 = 1 - \alpha_1 = \beta_1 \geq \beta_1 \left(1 + \frac{c_1 \log T}{T}\right)^2 / 3$ . Next, let  $t_0 > 2$  be the first time that Condition (96) fails to hold and suppose that  $t_0 \leq \tau$ . It then follows that

$$
1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t _ {0} - 2} = 1 - \frac {\bar {\alpha} _ {t _ {0} - 1}}{\alpha_ {t _ {0} - 1}} \leq 1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t _ {0} - 1} <   \frac {1}{3} \beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {t _ {0}} \leq \frac {1}{2} \beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {t _ {0} - 1} <   \frac {1}{2}, \tag {97}
$$

where the last inequality result from (95) and the assumption  $t_0 \leq \tau$ . This taken together with the assumptions (96) and  $t_0 \leq \tau$  implies that

$$
\frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t _ {0} - 1}) \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t _ {0} - 1}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t _ {0} - 2}} \geq \frac {\frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T} \beta_ {1} \min \left\{\left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {t _ {0} - 1} , 1 \right\} \cdot \left(1 - \frac {1}{2}\right)}{\frac {1}{2} \beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {t _ {0} - 1}} = \frac {\frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T} \beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {t _ {0} - 1}}{\beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {t _ {0} - 1}} = \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}.
$$

As a result, we can further derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} 1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t _ {0} - 1} = 1 - \alpha_ {t _ {0} - 1} \bar {\alpha} _ {t _ {0} - 2} = 1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t _ {0} - 2} + (1 - \alpha_ {t _ {0} - 1}) \bar {\alpha} _ {t _ {0} - 2} \\ = \left(1 + \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t _ {0} - 1}) \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t _ {0} - 2}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t _ {0} - 2}}\right) (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t _ {0} - 2}) \\ \geq \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t _ {0} - 2}\right) \geq \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) \cdot \left\{\frac {1}{3} \beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {t _ {0} - 1} \right\} \\ = \frac {1}{3} \beta_ {1} \bigg (1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T} \bigg) ^ {t _ {0}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the penultimate line holds since (96) is first violated at  $t = t_0$ ; this, however, contradicts with the definition of  $t_0$ . Consequently, one must have  $t_0 > \tau$ , meaning that (96) holds for all  $t \leq \tau$ .

- We then turn attention to those  $t$  obeying  $t > \tau$ . In this case, it suffices to make the observation that

$$
1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t - 1} \geq 1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {\tau - 1} \geq \frac {1}{3} \beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {\tau} = \frac {\frac {1}{3} \beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {\tau + 1}}{1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}} \geq \frac {1}{4}, \tag {98}
$$

where the second and the third inequalities come from (96). Therefore, one obtains

$$
\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} \leq \frac {\frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}}{1 / 4} \leq \frac {4 c _ {1} \log T}{T}.
$$

The above arguments taken together establish property (39b).

In addition, it comes immediately from (39b) that

$$
1 \leq \frac {1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} = 1 + \frac {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} = 1 + \frac {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1} (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} \leq 1 + \frac {4 c _ {1} \log T}{T},
$$

thereby justifying property (39c).

Proof of property (39d). Turning attention to the second claim (39d), we note that for any  $t$  obeying  $t \geq \frac{T}{2} \gtrsim \frac{T}{\log T}$ , one has

$$
1 - \alpha_ {t} = \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T} \min  \left\{\beta_ {1} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {t}, 1 \right\} = \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}.
$$

This in turn allows one to deduce that

$$
\overline {{\alpha}} _ {T} \leq \prod_ {t: t \geq T / 2} \alpha_ {t} \leq \left(1 - \frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) ^ {T / 2} \leq \frac {1}{T ^ {c _ {2}}}
$$

for an arbitrarily large constant  $c_{2} > 0$

Proof of property (39e). Finally, it is easily seen from the Taylor expansion that the learning rates  $\{\alpha_{t}\}$  satisfy

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\frac {1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) ^ {d / 2} = \left(1 + \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) ^ {d / 2} \\ = 1 + \frac {d (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} + \frac {d (d - 2) (1 - \alpha_ {t}) ^ {2}}{8 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) ^ {2}} + O \bigg (d ^ {3} \Big (\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \Big) ^ {3} \bigg), \\ \end{array}
$$

provided that  $\frac{d(1 - \alpha_t)}{\alpha_t - \overline{\alpha}_t} \lesssim 1$ .

# A.3 Proof of Lemma 1

To establish this lemma, we first make the following claim, whose proof is deferred to the end of this subsection.

Claim 1. Consider any  $c_{5} \geq 2$  and suppose that  $c_{6} \geq 2c_{R}$ . There exists some  $x_{0} \in \mathbb{R}^{d}$  such that

$$
\left\| \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0} - y \right\| _ {2} \leq c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d (1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}) \log T} \quad a n d \tag {99a}
$$

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\| X _ {0} - x _ {0} \| _ {2} \leq \epsilon\right) \geq \left(\frac {\epsilon}{T ^ {2 \theta (y)}}\right) ^ {d} \quad \text {w i t h} \quad \epsilon = \frac {1}{T ^ {c _ {0} / 2}} \tag {99b}
$$

hold simultaneously, where  $c_0$  is defined in (22).

With the above claim in place, we are ready to prove Lemma 1. For notational simplicity, we let  $X$  represent a random vector whose distribution  $p_X(\cdot)$  obeys

$$
p _ {X} (x) = p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x \mid y). \tag {100}
$$

Consider the point  $x_0$  in Claim 1, and let us look at a set:

$$
\mathcal {E} := \left\{x: \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha_ {t}}} \| x - x _ {0} \| _ {2}} > 4 c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) \log T} \right\},
$$

where  $c_{5} \geq 2$  (see Claim 1). Combining this with property (99a) about  $x_{0}$  results in

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\| \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} X - y \| _ {2} > 5 c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \log T}\right) \leq \mathbb {P} (X \in \mathcal {E}). \tag {101}
$$

Consequently, everything boils down to bounding  $\mathbb{P}(X\in \mathcal{E})$ . Towards this, we first invoke the Bayes rule  $p_{X_0\mid X_t}(x\mid y)\propto p_{X_0}(x)p_{X_t\mid X_0}(y\mid x)$  to derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(X _ {0} \in \mathcal {E} \mid X _ {t} = y\right) = \frac {\int_ {x \in \mathcal {E}} p _ {X _ {0}} (x) p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (y \mid x) \mathrm {d} x}{\int_ {x} p _ {X _ {0}} (x) p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (y \mid x) \mathrm {d} x} \\ \leq \frac {\int_ {x \in \mathcal {E}} p _ {X _ {0}} (x) p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (y \mid x) \mathrm {d} x}{\int_ {x : \| x - x _ {0} \| _ {2} \leq \epsilon} p _ {X _ {0}} (x) p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (y \mid x) \mathrm {d} x} \\ \leq \frac {\sup  _ {x \in \mathcal {E}} p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (y \mid x)}{\inf  _ {x : \| x - x _ {0} \| _ {2} \leq \epsilon} p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (y \mid x)} \cdot \frac {\mathbb {P} \left(X _ {0} \in \mathcal {E}\right)}{\mathbb {P} \left(\| X _ {0} - x _ {0} \| _ {2} \leq \epsilon\right)}. \tag {102} \\ \end{array}
$$

To further bound this quantity, note that: in view of the definition of  $\mathcal{E}$  and expression (99a), one has

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sup  _ {x \in \mathcal {E}} p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (y \mid x) = \sup  _ {x: \| \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \| _ {2} > 4 c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) \log T}} p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (y \mid x) \\ \leq \sup_{x:||\sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}} x - y\|_{2} > 3c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1 - \overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}}p_{X_{t}\mid X_{0}}(y\mid x) \\ \leq \frac {1}{\left(2 \pi (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})\right) ^ {d / 2}} \exp \left(- \frac {9 c _ {5} ^ {2} \theta (y) d \log T}{2}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \inf  _ {x: \| x - x _ {0} \| _ {2} \leq \epsilon} p _ {X _ {t}} | X _ {0} (y | x) \geq \frac {1}{\left(2 \pi \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)\right) ^ {d / 2}} \inf  _ {x: \| x - x _ {0} \| _ {2} \leq \epsilon} \exp \left(- \frac {\| y - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)}\right) \\ \geq \frac {1}{\left(2 \pi (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})\right) ^ {d / 2}} \inf _ {x: \| x - x _ {0} \| _ {2} \leq \epsilon} \exp \left(- \frac {\| y - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}}} _ {t} x _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} - \frac {\| \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}}} _ {t} x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}}} _ {t} x _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) \\ \geq \frac {1}{\left(2 \pi (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})\right) ^ {d / 2}} \exp \left(- \frac {\| y - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} - \frac {\epsilon^ {2}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \geq \frac {1}{\left(2 \pi \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)\right) ^ {d / 2}} \exp \left(- c _ {5} ^ {2} \theta (y) d \log T - \frac {1}{T ^ {c _ {0}}} \frac {1}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) \\ \geq \frac {1}{\left(2 \pi \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)\right) ^ {d / 2}} \exp \left(- 2 c _ {3} ^ {2} \theta (y) d \log T\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second line is due to the elementary inequality  $\|a + b\|_2^2 \leq 2\|a\|_2^2 + 2\|b\|_2^2$ , the penultimate line relies on (99), and the last line holds true since  $1 - \overline{\alpha}_t \geq 1 - \alpha_1 = 1 / T^{c_0}$  (see (22)). Substitution of the above two displays into (102), we arrive at

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(X _ {0} \in \mathcal {E} \mid X _ {t} = y\right) \leq \exp \left(- 2. 5 c _ {5} ^ {2} \theta (y) d \log T\right) \cdot \frac {1}{\mathbb {P} \left(\| X _ {0} - x _ {0} \| _ {2} \leq \epsilon\right)} \\ \leq \exp \left(- 2. 5 c _ {5} ^ {2} \theta d \log T\right) \cdot \left(T ^ {2 \theta (y) + c _ {0} / 2}\right) ^ {d} \\ \leq \exp \left(- \left(2. 5 c _ {5} ^ {2} \theta (y) - 2 \theta (y) - c _ {0} / 2\right) d \log T\right), \tag {103} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second inequality invokes (99b). Substituting this into (101) and recalling the distribution (100) of  $X$ , we arrive at

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\| \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} X - y \| _ {2} > 5 c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d (1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}) \log T}\right) \leq \exp \left(- \left(2. 5 c _ {5} ^ {2} \theta (y) - 2 \theta (y) - c _ {0} / 2\right) d \log T\right) \\ \leq \exp \big (- c _ {5} ^ {2} \theta (y) d \log T \big), \\ \end{array}
$$

with the proviso that  $c_{5} \geq 2$  and  $c_{6} \geq c_{0}$  (so that  $\theta(y) \geq c_{6} \geq c_{0}$ ). This concludes the proof of the advertised result (42) when  $c_{5} \geq 2$  and  $c_{6} \geq 2c_{R} + c_{0}$ , as long as Claim 1 can be justified.

With the above result in place, it then follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \left| \left| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} X _ {0} \right| \right| _ {2} \mid X _ {t} = x _ {t} \right] \\ \leq 5c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1 - \overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T} +\mathbb{E}\left[ \left\| x_{t} - \sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}} X_{0}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{1}\left\{\| x_{t} - \sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}} X_{0}\|_{2}\geq 5c_{5}\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1 - \overline{\alpha}_{t})\log T}\right\} \Bigg|X_{t} = x_{t}\right] \\ \leq 5 c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \log T} + \int_ {5 c _ {5}} ^ {\infty} \sqrt {\theta (y) d \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \log T} \mathbb {P} \left(\left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} \geq \tau \mid X _ {t} = x _ {t}\right) \mathrm {d} \tau \\ \leq 5 c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \log T} + \int_ {5 c _ {5}} ^ {\infty} \exp \left(- \frac {\tau^ {2}}{2 5 \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right)}\right) \mathrm {d} \tau \\ \leq 5 c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \log T} + \exp \left(- c _ {5} ^ {2} \theta (y) d \log T\right) \\ \leq 6 c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \log T}, \\ \end{array}
$$

as claimed in (43a) by taking  $c_{5} = 2$ . The proofs for (43b), (43c) and (43d) follow from similar arguments and are hence omitted for the sake of brevity.

Proof of Claim 1. We prove this claim by contradiction. Specifically, suppose instead that: for every  $x$  obeying  $\| \sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_t} x - y \|_2 \leq c_5 \sqrt{\theta(y) d(1 - \overline{\alpha}_t) \log T}$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\| X _ {0} - x \| _ {2} \leq \epsilon\right) \leq \left(\frac {\epsilon}{2 T ^ {\theta (y)} R}\right) ^ {d} \quad \text {w i t h} \epsilon = \frac {1}{T ^ {c _ {0} / 2}}. \tag {104}
$$

Clearly, the choice of  $\epsilon$  ensures that  $\epsilon < \frac{1}{2}\sqrt{d(1 - \overline{\alpha}_t)\log T}$ . In the following, we would like to show that this assumption leads to contradiction.

First of all, let us look at  $p_{X_t}$ , which obeys

$$
\begin{array}{l} p _ {X _ {t}} (y) = \int_ {x} p _ {X _ {0}} (x) p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (y \mid x) \mathrm {d} x \\ = \int_ {x: \| \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha_ {t}}} x - y \| _ {2} \geq c _ {5}} \sqrt {\theta (y) d \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right) \log T}} p _ {X _ {0}} (x) p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (y \mid x) \mathrm {d} x \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
+ \int_ {x: \| \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}} x - y \| _ {2} <   c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right) \log T} p _ {X _ {0}} (x) p _ {X _ {t}} | X _ {0} (y | x) \mathrm {d} x. \tag {105}
$$

To further control (105), we make two observations:

1) The first term on the right-hand side of (105) can be bounded by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \int_ {x: \| \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x - y \| _ {2} \geq c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) \log T}} p _ {X _ {0}} (x) p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (y \mid x) \mathrm {d} x \\ \leq \sup  _ {z: \| z \| _ {2} \geq c _ {5}} \sqrt {\theta (y) d (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) \log T} \frac {1}{(2 \pi (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})) ^ {d / 2}} \exp \left(- \frac {\| z \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})}\right) \\ <   \frac {1}{2} \exp (- \theta (y) d \log T), \tag {106} \\ \end{array}
$$

provided that  $c_{5} \geq 2$  and  $c_{6} > 0$  is large enough (note that  $\theta(y) \geq c_{6}$ ). Here, we have used  $X_{t} \stackrel{\mathrm{(i)}}{=} \sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t}} X_{0} + \sqrt{1 - \overline{\alpha}_{t}} W$  with  $W \sim \mathcal{N}(0, I_{d})$  as well as standard properties about Gaussian distributions.

2) Regarding the second term on the right-hand side of (105), let us construct an epsilon-net  $\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon} = \{z_{i}\}$  for the following set

$$
\left\{x: \| \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x - y \| _ {2} \leq c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) \log T} \text {a n d} \| x \| _ {2} \leq R \right\},
$$

so that for each  $x$  in this set, one can find a vector  $z_{i}\in \mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}$  such that  $\| x - z_i\| _2\leq \epsilon$ . Clearly, we can choose  $\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}$  so that its cardinality obeys  $|\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}|\leq (2R / \epsilon)^{d}$ . Define  $\mathcal{B}_i\coloneqq \{x\mid \| x - z_i\| _2\leq \epsilon \}$  for each  $z_{i}\in \mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}$ . Armed with these sets, we can derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \int_ {x: \| \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x - y \| _ {2} <   c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta (y) d \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right) \log T}} p _ {X _ {0}} (x) p _ {X _ {t} | X _ {0}} (y | x) \mathrm {d} x \leq \left(2 \pi \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)\right) ^ {- d / 2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {| \mathcal {N} _ {\epsilon} |} \mathbb {P} \left(X _ {0} \in \mathcal {B} _ {i}\right) \\ \leq \left(2 \pi \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)\right) ^ {- d / 2} \left(\frac {\epsilon}{2 T ^ {2 \theta (y)} R}\right) ^ {d} \left(\frac {2 R}{\epsilon}\right) ^ {d} \\ <   \frac {1}{2} \exp \big (- \theta (y) d \log T \big), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the penultimate step comes from the assumption (104).

The above results taken collectively lead to

$$
p _ {X _ {t}} (y) <   \exp (- \theta (y) d \log T), \tag {107}
$$

thus contradicting the definition (41) of  $\theta(y)$ .

Consequently, we have proven the existence of  $x$  obeying  $\| \sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_t} x - y\| _2\leq c_5\sqrt{\theta(y)d(1 - \overline{\alpha}_t)\log T}$  and

$$
\mathbb {P} (\| X _ {0} - x \| _ {2} \leq \epsilon) > \left(\frac {\epsilon}{2 T ^ {\theta (y)} R}\right) ^ {d} \geq \left(\frac {\epsilon}{T ^ {2 \theta (y)}}\right) ^ {d},
$$

provided that  $\theta(y) \geq c_6 \geq 2c_R$ . This completes the proof of Claim 1.

# A.4 Proof of Lemma 2

For notational convenience, let us denote  $\widehat{x}_t = x_t / \sqrt{\alpha_t}$  throughout the proof. As a key step of the proof, we note that for any  $x\in \mathbb{R}^d$

$$
p _ {X _ {t - 1}} (x) = \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0}} (x _ {0}) p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {0}} (x \mid x _ {0}) \mathrm {d} x _ {0}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0}} \left(x _ {0}\right) p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} \left(x _ {t} \mid x _ {0}\right) \cdot \frac {p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {0}} \left(x \mid x _ {0}\right)}{p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} \left(x _ {t} \mid x _ {0}\right)} \mathrm {d} x _ {0} \\ = p _ {X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t}\right) \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x _ {t}\right) \cdot \frac {p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {0}} \left(x \mid x _ {0}\right)}{p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} \left(x _ {t} \mid x _ {0}\right)} \mathrm {d} x _ {0}, \tag {108} \\ \end{array}
$$

thus establishing a link between  $p_{X_{t-1}}(x)$  and  $p_{X_t}(x_t)$ . Consequently, in order to control  $p_{X_{t-1}}(x)$ , it is helpful to first look at the density ratio  $\frac{p_{X_{t-1}}|x_0(x|x_0)}{p_{X_t}|x_0(x_t|x_0)}$ , which we accomplish in the sequel.

Recall that  $X_{t} \stackrel{\mathrm{d}}{=} \sqrt{\overline{\alpha}} X_{0} + \sqrt{1 - \overline{\alpha}} W$  with  $W \sim \mathcal{N}(0, I_{d})$ . In what follows, let us consider any  $x_{t}, x_{0} \in \mathbb{R}^{d}$  and any  $x$  obeying

$$
\left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} \leq c _ {3} \sqrt {d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \log T} \tag {109}
$$

for some constant  $c_3 > 0$ . The density ratio of interest satisfies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {0}} (x \mid x _ {0})}{p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (x _ {t} \mid x _ {0})} = \left(\frac {1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}\right) ^ {d / 2} \exp \left(\frac {\left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} - \frac {\left\| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1})}\right) \\ \leq \exp \left(\frac {d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) + \left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + 2 \left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2}}{2 (1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t - 1})} + \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t - 1}) ^ {2}}\right), \tag {110} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality follows from the two relations below:

$$
\log \frac {1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} = \log \left(1 + \frac {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1} (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}\right) \leq \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}
$$

and  $\left|\frac{\left\|x_t - \sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_t}x_0\right\|_2^2}{2(1 - \overline{\alpha}_t)} -\frac{\left\|x - \sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{t - 1}}x_0\right\|_2^2}{2(1 - \overline{\alpha}_{t - 1})}\right|$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \left| \frac {\left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} - \frac {\left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + \left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} - 2 \left\langle \widehat {x} _ {t} - x , \widehat {x} _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} x _ {0} \right\rangle}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1})} \right| \\ \leq \frac {\left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1})} + \frac {\left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} \left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} + \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \alpha_ {t} (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1})} \\ \leq \frac {\left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + 2 \left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} \left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1})} + \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}) ^ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Next, let us define the following set given  $x_{t}$ :

$$
\widetilde {\mathcal {E}} := \left\{x _ {0}: \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} \leq c _ {4} \sqrt {d (1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}) \log T} \right\} \tag {111}
$$

for some large enough numerical constant  $c_4 > 0$ , and we shall look at  $\tilde{\mathcal{E}}$  and  $\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^c$  separately. Towards this, we make the following observations:

- For any  $x_0 \in \widetilde{\mathcal{E}}$ , one can utilize (111) and (109) to deduce that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) + \left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + 2 \left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha_ {t}}}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1})} + \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}) ^ {2}} \\ \lesssim \frac {d (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} + \frac {d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \log T + d \sqrt {(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) (1 - \alpha_ {t})} \log T}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} + \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) ^ {2} d (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) \log T}{(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}) ^ {2}} \\ \lesssim (d \log T) \left\{\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} + \sqrt {\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}} \sqrt {\frac {1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}} + \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}\right) ^ {2} \right\} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\lesssim d \sqrt {\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}} \log T, \tag {112}
$$

where the last inequality makes use of the facts  $\frac{1 - \alpha_t}{1 - \overline{\alpha}_{t - 1}}\leq 1$  (cf. (39b)) and

$$
\frac {1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} = \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} + \frac {\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} = \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} + \alpha_ {t} \leq 2. \tag {113}
$$

Moreover, the properties (39) of the stepsizes tell us that

$$
d \sqrt {\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}} \log T \lesssim d \sqrt {\frac {\log^ {3} T}{T}} \leq c _ {1 0}
$$

for some small enough constant  $c_{10} > 0$ , as long as  $T \geq c_{11}d^{2}\log^{3}T$  for some sufficiently large constant  $c_{11} > 0$ . Taking this together with (110) and (112) reveals that

$$
\frac {p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {0}} (x \mid x _ {0})}{p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (x _ {t} \mid x _ {0})} = 1 + O \left(d \sqrt {\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t - 1}}} \log T\right), \tag {114}
$$

with the proviso that (109) holds and  $x_0 \in \widetilde{\mathcal{E}}$ .

- Instead, if  $x_0 \notin \widetilde{\mathcal{E}}$ , then one can obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) + \left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + 2 \left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1})} + \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}) ^ {2}} \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(i)}} {\leq} \frac {d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) + \left(1 + \frac {1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}{1 - \alpha_ {t}}\right) \left\| \widehat {x} _ {t} - x \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1})} + \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}) ^ {2}} \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(i i)}} {\leq} \frac {d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) + c _ {3} \left(1 + \frac {1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}{1 - \alpha_ {t}}\right) d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \log T}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1})} + \frac {2 (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}) ^ {2}} \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(i i i)}} {\leq} \frac {c _ {3} d \left(1 - \alpha_ {t} + 1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}\right) \log T}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} \cdot \frac {\left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{c _ {4} d (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}) \log T} + \frac {2 (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}) ^ {2}} \\ \leq \frac {2 c _ {3}}{c _ {4}} \cdot \frac {\left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} + \frac {8 c _ {1} \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \log T}{T (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1})} \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(i v)}} {\leq} \left(\frac {2 c _ {3}}{c _ {4}} + \frac {8 c _ {1} \log T}{T}\right) \frac {2 \left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha_ {t}}}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \leq \frac {8 c _ {3}}{c _ {4}} \frac {\left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha_ {t}}}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where (i) comes from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, (ii) is valid due to the assumption on  $\| \widehat{x}_t - x\| _2$  (iii) follows from the definition of  $\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}$ , and (iv) is a consequence of (113). Substitution into (110) leads to

$$
\frac {p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {0}} (x \mid x _ {0})}{p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} (x _ {t} \mid x _ {0})} \leq \exp \left(\frac {8 c _ {3}}{c _ {4}} \frac {\left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha_ {t}}}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right), \tag {115}
$$

provided that (109) holds and  $x_0 \notin \widetilde{\mathcal{E}}$ .

In light of the above calculations, we can invoke (116) to demonstrate that: for any  $x$  obeying (109),

$$
\begin{array}{l} p _ {X _ {t - 1}} (x) = p _ {X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t}\right) \left(\int_ {x _ {0} \in \widetilde {\mathcal {E}}} + \int_ {x _ {0} \notin \widetilde {\mathcal {E}}}\right) p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x _ {t}\right) \cdot \frac {p _ {X _ {t - 1} \mid X _ {0}} \left(x \mid x _ {0}\right)}{p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} \left(x _ {t} \mid x _ {0}\right)} d x _ {0} \\ = p _ {X _ {t}} (x _ {t}) \int_ {x _ {0} \in \widetilde {\mathcal {E}}} \left(1 + O \left(d \sqrt {\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}} \log T\right)\right) p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x _ {t}) \mathrm {d} x _ {0} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
+ p _ {X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t}\right) \int_ {x _ {0} \notin \tilde {\mathcal {E}}} O \left(\exp \left(\frac {8 c _ {3}}{c _ {4}} \frac {\left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right)\right) p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x _ {t}\right) d x _ {0}. \tag {116}
$$

By virtue of Lemma 1, if  $-\log p_{X_t}(x_t) \leq \frac{1}{2} c_6 d \log T$  for some large constant  $c_6 > 0$ , then it holds that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left\{\left\| \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} X _ {0} - x _ {t} \right\| _ {2} \geq 5 c _ {5} \sqrt {d (1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}) \log T} \mid X _ {t} = x _ {t} \right\} \leq \exp \left(- c _ {5} ^ {2} c _ {6} d \log T\right) \tag {117}
$$

for any  $c_{5} \geq 2$ . Some elementary calculation then reveals that

$$
\int_ {x _ {0} \notin \tilde {\mathcal {E}}} \exp \left(\frac {8 c _ {3}}{c _ {4}} \frac {\left\| x _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha_ {t}}}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x _ {t}\right) \mathrm {d} x _ {0} \lesssim \frac {1}{T ^ {c _ {0}}} \tag {118}
$$

with  $c_{0}$  defined in (22), provided that  $c_{4} / c_{3}$  is sufficiently large. Hence, substituting it into (116) demonstrates that, for any  $x$  satisfying (109),

$$
\begin{array}{l} p _ {X _ {t - 1}} (x) = \left(1 + O \left(d \sqrt {\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t - 1}}} \log T\right)\right) \left(1 - o (1)\right) p _ {X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t}\right) + O \left(\frac {1}{T ^ {c _ {0}}}\right) p _ {X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t}\right) (119) \\ = \left(1 + O \left(d \sqrt {\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}} \log T + \frac {1}{T ^ {c _ {0}}}\right)\right) p _ {X _ {t}} (x _ {t}) \\ = \left(1 + O \left(d \sqrt {\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t - 1}}} \log T\right)\right) p _ {X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t}\right) (120) \\ \in \left[ \frac {1}{2} p _ {X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t}\right), \frac {3}{2} p _ {X _ {t}} \left(x _ {t}\right) \right], (121) \\ \end{array}
$$

where the penultimate inequality holds since (according to (22))

$$
\sqrt {\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}}} \geq \sqrt {1 - \alpha_ {t}} = \sqrt {\beta_ {t}} \geq \sqrt {\frac {c _ {1} \log T}{T ^ {c _ {0} + 1}}},
$$

and the last inequality holds due to (39b) and the condition that  $T / (d^2 \log^3 T)$  is sufficiently large. In other words, (121) reveals that  $p_{X_{t-1}}(x)$  is sufficiently close to  $p_{X_t}(x_t)$ .

We are now ready to establish our claim. In view of the assumption (44), we have

$$
\left\| x _ {t} (\gamma) - \widehat {x} _ {t} \right\| _ {2} = \left\| \gamma x _ {t - 1} + (1 - \gamma) \widehat {x} _ {t} - \widehat {x} _ {t} \right\| _ {2} = \gamma \left\| x _ {t - 1} - \widehat {x} _ {t} \right\| _ {2} \leq c _ {3} \sqrt {d (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \log T}.
$$

Therefore, taking  $x$  to be  $x_{t}(\gamma)$  in (121) tells us that: if  $-\log p_{X_t}(x_t) \leq \frac{1}{2} c_6 d \log T$ , then

$$
- \log p _ {X _ {t - 1}} (x _ {t} (\gamma)) \leq - \log p _ {X _ {t}} (x _ {t}) + \log 2 \leq c _ {6} d \log T
$$

as claimed.

# A.5 Proof of Lemma 3

Recognizing that  $Y_{T} \sim \mathcal{N}(0, I_{d})$  and that  $X_{T} \stackrel{\mathrm{d}}{=} \sqrt{\overline{\alpha}_{T}} X_{0} + \sqrt{1 - \overline{\alpha}_{T}} \overline{W}_{t}$  with  $\overline{W}_{t} \sim \mathcal{N}(0, I_{d})$  (independent from  $X_{0}$ ), one has

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathsf {K L} \left(p _ {X _ {T}} \| p _ {Y _ {T}}\right) = \int p _ {X _ {T}} (x) \log \frac {p _ {X _ {T}} (x)}{p _ {Y _ {T}} (x)} \mathrm {d} x \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(i)}} {=} \int p _ {X _ {T}} (x) \log \frac {\int_ {y : \| y \| _ {2} \leq \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha_ {T}}} T ^ {c _ {R}}}} p _ {\sqrt {\overline {{\alpha_ {T}}}} X _ {0}} (y) p _ {\sqrt {1 - \overline {{\alpha_ {T}}}} \overline {{W}} _ {t}} (x - y) \mathrm {d} y}{p _ {Y _ {T}} (x)} \mathrm {d} x \\ \leq \int p _ {X _ {T}} (x) \log \frac {\sup _ {y : \| y \| _ {2} \leq \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {T}} T ^ {c _ {R}}} p _ {\sqrt {1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {T}} \overline {{W}} _ {t}} (x - y)}{p _ {Y _ {T}} (x)} \mathrm {d} x \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \int p _ {X _ {T}} (x) \left(- d / 2 \log \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {T}\right) + \sup  _ {y: \| y \| _ {2} \leq \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {T}} T ^ {c} R} \left(- \frac {\| x - y \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {T}\right)} + \frac {\| x \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2}\right) \mathrm {d} x \right. \\ \stackrel {\text {(i i)}} {\leq} \int p _ {X _ {T}} (x) \left(- d / 2 \log \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {T}\right) + \| x \| _ {2} \sup  _ {y: \| y \| _ {2} \leq \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {T}} T ^ {c _ {R}}} \frac {\| y \| _ {2}}{1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {T}}\right) \mathrm {d} x \\ \leq - d / 2 \log (1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {T}) + \frac {\sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {T}} T ^ {c _ {R}}}{2 (1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {T})} \mathbb {E} [ \| X _ {T} \| _ {2} ] \\ \stackrel {(\mathrm {i i i})} {\lesssim} \bar {\alpha} _ {T} d + \frac {\sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {T}} T ^ {c _ {R}}}{2 (1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {T})} \left(\sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {T}} T ^ {c _ {R}} + \sqrt {d}\right) \stackrel {(\mathrm {i v})} {\lesssim} \frac {1}{T ^ {2 0 0}}, \tag {122} \\ \end{array}
$$

where (i) arises from the assumption that  $\| X_0\| _2\leq T^{c_R}$ , (ii) applies the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, (iii) holds true since

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \| X _ {T} \| _ {2} \right] \leq \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {T}} \| X _ {0} \| _ {2} + \mathbb {E} \left[ \| \overline {{W}} _ {t} \| _ {2} \right] \leq \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {T}} T ^ {c _ {R}} + \sqrt {\mathbb {E} \left[ \| \overline {{W}} _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \right]} \leq \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {T}} T ^ {c _ {R}} + \sqrt {d},
$$

and (iv) makes use of (39d) given that  $c_{2} \geq 1000$ . The proof is thus completed by invoking the Pinsker inequality (Tsybakov, 2009, Lemma 2.5).

# B Proof of auxiliary lemmas for the ODE-based sampler

# B.1 Proof of Lemma 4

# B.1.1 Proof of relations (57) and (58a)

Recall the definition of  $\phi_t$  and  $\phi_t^\star$  in (51), and introduce the following vector:

$$
\begin{array}{l} u := x - \phi_ {t} (x) = x - \phi_ {t} ^ {\star} (x) + \phi_ {t} ^ {\star} (x) - \phi_ {t} (x) \\ = \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{2 \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right)} \int_ {x _ {0}} \left(x - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0}\right) p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x\right) d x _ {0} - \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{2} \left(s _ {t} (x) - s _ {t} ^ {\star} (x)\right). \tag {123} \\ \end{array}
$$

The proof is composed of the following steps.

Step 1: decomposing  $p_{\sqrt{\alpha_t} X_{t-1}}(\phi_t(x)) / p_{X_t}(x)$ . Recognizing that

$$
X _ {t} \stackrel {\mathrm {d}} {=} \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} X _ {0} + \sqrt {1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} W \quad \text {w i t h} W \sim \mathcal {N} (0, I _ {d}) \tag {124}
$$

and making use of the Bayes rule, we can express the conditional distribution  $p_{X_0 \mid X_t}(\phi_t(x))$  as

$$
p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x\right) = \frac {p _ {X _ {0}} \left(x _ {0}\right)}{p _ {X _ {t}} (x)} p _ {X _ {t} \mid X _ {0}} \left(x \mid x _ {0}\right) = \frac {p _ {X _ {0}} \left(x _ {0}\right)}{p _ {X _ {t}} (x)} \cdot \frac {1}{\left(2 \pi \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)\right) ^ {d / 2}} \exp \left(- \frac {\left\| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)}\right). \tag {125}
$$

Moreover, it follows from (124) that

$$
\sqrt {\alpha_ {t}} X _ {t - 1} \stackrel {{\mathrm {d}}} {{=}} \sqrt {\alpha_ {t}} \left(\sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} X _ {0} + \sqrt {1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t - 1}} W\right) = \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} X _ {0} + \sqrt {\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} W. \tag {126}
$$

These taken together allow one to rewrite  $p_{\sqrt{\alpha_t} X_{t-1}}$  such that:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {p _ {\sqrt {\alpha_ {t}} X _ {t - 1}} \left(\phi_ {t} (x)\right)}{p _ {X _ {t}} (x)} \stackrel {\mathrm {(i)}} {=} \frac {1}{p _ {X _ {t}} (x)} \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0}} (x _ {0}) \frac {1}{\left(2 \pi (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})\right) ^ {d / 2}} \exp \left(- \frac {\left\| \phi_ {t} (x) - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})}\right) \mathrm {d} x _ {0} \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(i i)}} {=} \frac {1}{p _ {X _ {t}} (x)} \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0}} (x _ {0}) \frac {1}{\left(2 \pi (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})\right) ^ {d / 2}} \exp \left(- \frac {\left\| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \cdot \exp \left(- \frac {\left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) \left\| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right) \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)} - \frac {\| u \| _ {2} ^ {2} - 2 u ^ {\top} \left(x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0}\right)}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)}\right) d x _ {0} \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(i i i)}} {=} \left(\frac {1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) ^ {d / 2} \cdot \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x). \\ \exp \left(- \frac {\left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) \left\| x - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right)} - \frac {\left\| u \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} - 2 u ^ {\top} \left(x - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0}\right)}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right)}\right) d x _ {0} \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(i v)}} {=} \left\{1 + \frac {d (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} + O \left(d ^ {2} \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) ^ {2}\right) \right\}. \\ \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x\right) \exp \left(- \frac {\left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) \left\| x - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right)} - \frac {\left\| u \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} - 2 u ^ {\top} \left(x - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0}\right)}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right)}\right) d x _ {0}. \tag {127} \\ \end{array}
$$

Here, identity (i) holds due to (126) and hence

$$
p _ {\sqrt {\alpha_ {t}} X _ {t - 1}} (x) = \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0}} (x _ {0}) p _ {\sqrt {\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} W} \left(x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0}\right) d x _ {0};
$$

identity (ii) follows from (123) and elementary algebra; relation (iii) is a consequence of the Bayes rule (125); and relation (iv) results from (39e).

Step 2: controlling the integral in the decomposition (127). In order to further control the right-hand side of expression (127), we need to evaluate the integral in (127). To this end, we make a few observations.

- To begin with, Lemma 1 tells us that

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left\| \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} X _ {0} - x \right\| _ {2} > 5 c _ {5} \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \log T} \mid X _ {t} = x\right) \leq \exp \left(- c _ {5} ^ {2} \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T\right) \tag {128a}
$$

for any quantity  $c_{5} \geq 2$ , provided that  $c_{6} \geq 2c_{R} + c_{0}$ .

- A little algebra based on this relation allows one to bound  $u$  (cf. (123)) as follows:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| u \right\| _ {2} \leq \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{2} \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) + \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{2 \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)} \mathbb {E} \left[ \left\| \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} X _ {0} - x \right\| _ {2} \mid X _ {t} = x \right] \\ \leq \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{2} \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) + \frac {6 \left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right)}{1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}} \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \log T}, \tag {128b} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality arises from Lemma 1.

Next, let us define

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {c} ^ {\text {t y p i c a l}} := \left\{x _ {0}: \left\| x - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} \leq 5 c \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \log T} \right\} \tag {129}
$$

for any quantity  $c > 0$ . Then for any  $x_0 \in \mathcal{E}_c^{\mathrm{typical}}$ , it is clearly seen from (128) and (39) that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) \left\| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha_ {t}}}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right) \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)} \leq \frac {2 5 c ^ {2}}{2} \frac {\left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \leq \frac {1 0 0 c _ {1} c ^ {2} \theta_ {t} (x) d \log^ {2} T}{T}; (130a) \\ \frac {\left\| u \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right)} \leq \frac {\left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) ^ {2}}{4 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right)} \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) ^ {2} + \frac {3 6 \left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) ^ {2}}{\left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \left(\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right)} \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T (130b) \\ \leq \frac {2 c _ {1} ^ {2} \log^ {2} T}{T ^ {2}} \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) ^ {2} + \frac {2 3 0 4 c _ {1} ^ {2}}{T ^ {2}} \theta_ {t} (x) d \log^ {3} T, \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\left| \frac {u ^ {\top} (x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha_ {t}}}} x _ {0})}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \right| \leq \frac {\| u \| _ {2} \| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \| _ {2}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \frac {5 c (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t})} \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d (1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}) \log T} + \frac {3 0 c (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T}{\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}} (130c) \\ \leq \frac {2 0 c c _ {1}}{T} \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \log^ {3} T} + \frac {2 4 0 c c _ {1} \theta_ {t} (x) d \log^ {2} T}{T}. (130d) \\ \end{array}
$$

As a consequence, for any  $x_0 \in \mathcal{E}_c^{\mathrm{typical}}$  for  $c \geq 2$ , we have seen from (130d) and (39) that

$$
\begin{array}{l} - \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} - \frac {\| u \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} + \frac {u ^ {\top} (x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0})}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \leq \frac {u ^ {\top} (x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0})}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \\ \leq \frac {5 c (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t})} \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \log T} + \frac {3 0 c (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T}{\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}} (131) \\ \leq \frac {2 0 c c _ {1}}{T} \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d \log^ {3} T} + \frac {2 4 0 c c _ {1}}{T} \theta_ {t} (x) d \log^ {2} T \leq c \theta_ {t} (x) d, (132) \\ \end{array}
$$

provided that

$$
\frac {4 0 c _ {1} \varepsilon_ {\mathrm {s c o r e} , t} (x) \log^ {\frac {3}{2}} T}{T} \leq \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d} \quad \text {a n d} \quad T \geq 4 8 0 c _ {1} \log^ {2} T.
$$

Step 2(a): proof of relation (57). Substituting (131) into (127) and making use of (39) under our assumption on  $T$  yield

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {p _ {\sqrt {\alpha_ {t}} X _ {t - 1}} \left(\phi_ {t} (x)\right)}{p _ {X _ {t}} (x)} \leq 2 \exp \left(\frac {5 c (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d \log T} + \frac {3 0 c (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T\right) \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) d x _ {0} \\ \leq 2 \exp \left(\frac {5 c (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} \varepsilon_ {\mathsf {s c o r e}, t} (x) \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d \log T} + \frac {3 0 c (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

thus establishing (57) by taking  $c = 2$ .

Step 2(b): proof of relation (58a). Suppose now that

$$
C _ {1 0} \frac {\theta_ {t} (x) d \log^ {2} T + \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e} , t} (x) \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d \log^ {3} T}}{T} \leq 1 \tag {133}
$$

holds for some large enough constant  $C_{10} > 0$ . Under this additional condition, it can be easily verified that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| - \frac {\left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) \left\| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right) \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)} - \frac {\| u \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)} + \frac {u ^ {\top} \left(x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0}\right)}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \right| \\ \leq c _ {1 0} \left(\theta_ {t} (x) d \log T + \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d \log T}\right) \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}} \tag {134} \\ \end{array}
$$

for any  $x_0 \in \mathcal{E}_2^{\mathrm{typical}}$  (with  $c = 2$ ), where  $c_{10} > 0$  is some sufficiently small constant. Therefore, the Taylor expansion  $e^{-z} = 1 - z + O(z^{2})$  (for all  $|z| < 1$ ) gives

$$
\begin{array}{l} \exp \left(- \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \big \| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \big \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} - \frac {\| u \| _ {2} ^ {2} - 2 u ^ {\top} (x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0})}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})}\right) \\ = 1 - \frac {\left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) \left\| x - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right) \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right)} + \frac {u ^ {\top} \left(x - \sqrt {\bar {\alpha} _ {t}} x _ {0}\right)}{\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}} + O \left(\left(\theta_ {t} (x) ^ {2} d ^ {2} \log^ {2} T + \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) ^ {2} \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T\right) \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}}\right) ^ {2}\right) \tag {135} \\ \end{array}
$$

for any  $x_0 \in \mathcal{E}_2^{\text{typical}}$ , which invokes (134) and (130b) (under the assumption (133)). Combine (135) and (132) to show that

$$
\int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) \exp \left(- \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha_ {t}}}} x _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} - \frac {\| u \| _ {2} ^ {2} - 2 u ^ {\top} (x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0})}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})}\right) d x _ {0}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = \left(\int_ {x _ {0} \in \mathcal {E} _ {2}} + \int_ {x _ {0} \notin \mathcal {E} _ {2}}\right) p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) \exp \left(- \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} - \frac {\| u \| _ {2} ^ {2} - 2 u ^ {\top} (x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0})}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})}\right) d x _ {0} \\ = \int_ {x _ {0} \in \mathcal {E} _ {2}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x\right) \left(1 - \frac {\left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) \left\| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right) \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)} + \frac {u ^ {\top} \left(x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0}\right)}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) d x _ {0} \\ + O \left(\left(\theta_ {t} (x) ^ {2} d ^ {2} \log^ {2} T + \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) ^ {2} \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T\right) \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) ^ {2}\right) + O \left(\sum_ {c = 3} ^ {\infty} \int_ {x _ {0} \in \mathcal {E} _ {c} \backslash \mathcal {E} _ {c - 1}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) \exp (c \theta_ {t} (x) d) d x _ {0}\right) \\ = 1 - \frac {\left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) \left(\int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x\right) \| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \mathrm {d} x _ {0} - \left\| \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x\right) \left(x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0}\right) \mathrm {d} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}\right)}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right) \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)} \\ + O \left(\theta_ {t} (x) ^ {2} d ^ {2} \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) ^ {2} \log^ {2} T + \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d \log T} \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right)\right) + O \left(\exp \left(- \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T\right)\right) \\ = 1 - \frac {\left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) \left(\int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x\right) \| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \mathrm {d} x _ {0} - \left\| \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x\right) \left(x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0}\right) \mathrm {d} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}\right)}{2 \left(\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right) \left(1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}\right)} \\ + O \left(\theta_ {t} (x) ^ {2} d ^ {2} \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}}\right) ^ {2} \log^ {2} T + \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e}, t} (x) \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (x) d \log T} \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}}\right)\right), \tag {136} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the penultimate relation holds since, according to (128a),

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {c = 3} ^ {\infty} \int_ {x _ {0} \in \mathcal {E} _ {c} \backslash \mathcal {E} _ {c - 1}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) \exp (c \theta_ {t} (x) d) d x _ {0} \leq \sum_ {c = 3} ^ {\infty} \exp (- c ^ {2} \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T) \exp (c \theta_ {t} (x) d) \\ \leq \sum_ {c = 3} ^ {\infty} \exp \left(- \frac {1}{2} c ^ {2} \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T\right) \leq \exp \big (- \theta_ {t} (x) d \log T \big), \\ \end{array}
$$

and the last line in (136) again utilizes (39) and the fact that  $\theta_t(x) \geq c_6$  for some large enough constant  $c_6 > 0$ .

Putting (136) and (127) together yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac{p_{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}X_{t - 1}}\big(\phi_{t}(x)\big)}{p_{X_{t}}(x)} = 1 + \frac{d(1 - \alpha_{t})}{2(\alpha_{t} - \overline{\alpha}_{t})} +O\Bigg(\theta_{t}(x)^{2}d^{2}\Big(\frac{1 - \alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t} - \overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)^{2}\log^{2}T + \varepsilon_{\text{score},t}(x)\sqrt{\theta_{t}(x)d\log T}\Big(\frac{1 - \alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t} - \overline{\alpha}_{t}}\Big)\Big) - \\ \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \big (\int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) \| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \mathrm {d} x _ {0} - \big \| \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) \big (x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \big) \mathrm {d} x _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \big)}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} \\ \end{array}
$$

as claimed.

# B.1.2 Proof of relation (58b)

Consider any random vector  $Y$ . To understand the density ratio  $p_{\phi_t(Y)}(\phi_t(x)) / p_Y(x)$ , we make note of the transformation

$$
p _ {\phi_ {t} (Y)} \left(\phi_ {t} (x)\right) = \det  \left(\frac {\partial \phi_ {t} (x)}{\partial x}\right) ^ {- 1} p _ {Y} (x), \tag {137a}
$$

$$
p _ {\phi_ {t} ^ {*} (Y)} \left(\phi_ {t} ^ {\star} (x)\right) = \det  \left(\frac {\partial \phi_ {t} ^ {\star} (x)}{\partial x}\right) ^ {- 1} p _ {Y} (x), \tag {137b}
$$

where  $\frac{\partial\phi_t(x)}{\partial x}$  and  $\frac{\partial\phi_t^*(x)}{\partial x}$  denote the Jacobian matrices. It thus suffices to control the quantity  $\operatorname*{det}\left(\frac{\partial\phi_t(x)}{\partial x}\right)^{-1}$ .

To begin with, recall from (51) and (36) that

$$
\phi_ {t} ^ {\star} (x) = x - \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} g _ {t} (x).
$$

As a result, one can use (37) and (38) to derive

$$
I - \frac {\partial \phi_ {t} ^ {\star} (x)}{\partial x} = \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} J _ {t} (x) = \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} \left\{I + \frac {1}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \left\{\mathbb {E} \big [ X _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} X _ {0} \mid X _ {t} = x \big ] \Big (\mathbb {E} \big [ X _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} X _ {0} \mid X _ {t} = x \big ] \Big) ^ {\top} \right. \right.
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. \left. - \mathbb {E} \left[ \left(X _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} X _ {0}\right) \left(X _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} X _ {0}\right) ^ {\top} \mid X _ {t} = x \right] \right\} \right\} \\ =: \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{2 \left(1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}\right)} \left\{I + \frac {1}{1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}} B \right\}. \tag {138} \\ \end{array}
$$

This allows one to show that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {T r} \left(I - \frac {\partial \phi_ {t} ^ {\star} (x)}{\partial x}\right) = \frac {d (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} + \\ \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \left(\left\| \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x\right) \left(x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0}\right) \mathrm {d} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} - \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} \left(x _ {0} \mid x\right) \| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \mathrm {d} x _ {0}\right)}{2 (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) ^ {2}}. \tag {139a} \\ \end{array}
$$

Moreover, the matrix  $B$  defined in (138) satisfies

$$
\| B \| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq \left\| \mathbb {E} \left[ \left(X _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} X _ {0}\right) \left(X _ {t} - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} X _ {0}\right) ^ {\top} \mid X _ {t} = x \right] \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \leq \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) \left\| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \mathrm {d} x _ {0}
$$

due to Jensen's inequality. Taking this together with (138) and Lemma 1 reveals that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \frac {\partial \phi_ {t} ^ {\star} (x)}{\partial x} - I \right\| \leq \left\| \frac {\partial \phi_ {t} ^ {\star} (x)}{\partial x} - I \right\| _ {\mathrm {F}} \lesssim \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \left(\sqrt {d} + \frac {\int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) \| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \mathrm {d} x _ {0}}{1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}}\right) \\ \lesssim \frac {\theta_ {t} (x) d \left(1 - \alpha_ {t}\right) \log T}{1 - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}}. \tag {139b} \\ \end{array}
$$

Additionally, the Taylor expansion guarantees that for any  $A$  and  $\Delta$ ,

$$
\det (I + A + \Delta) = 1 + \operatorname {T r} (A) + O \left(\left(\operatorname {T r} (A)\right) ^ {2} + \| A \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + d ^ {3} \| A \| ^ {3} + d \| \Delta \|\right) \tag {140a}
$$

$$
\det (I + A + \Delta) ^ {- 1} = 1 - \operatorname {T r} (A) + O \left(\left(\operatorname {T r} (A)\right) ^ {2} + \| A \| _ {\mathrm {F}} ^ {2} + d ^ {3} \| A \| ^ {3} + d \| \Delta \|\right) \tag {140b}
$$

hold as long as  $d\| A\| +d\| \Delta \| \leq c_{11}$  for some small enough constant  $c_{11} > 0$ . The above properties taken collectively with (51) and (48) allow us to demonstrate that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {p _ {\phi_ {t} (Y)} (\phi_ {t} (x))}{p _ {Y} (x)} = \det  \left(\frac {\partial \phi_ {t} (x)}{\partial x}\right) ^ {- 1} = \left(\det  \left(\frac {\partial \phi_ {t} ^ {\star} (x)}{\partial x} + \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{2} \left[ J _ {s _ {t}} (x) - J _ {s _ {t} ^ {\star}} (x) \right]\right)\right) ^ {- 1} \\ = \left(\det  \left(I + \frac {\partial \phi_ {t} ^ {\star} (x)}{\partial x} - I + \frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{2} \left[ J _ {s _ {t}} (x) - J _ {s _ {t} ^ {\star}} (x) \right]\right)\right) ^ {- 1} \\ = 1 - \operatorname {T r} \left(\frac {\partial \phi_ {t} ^ {\star} (x)}{\partial x} - I\right) + O \left(\theta_ {t} (x) ^ {2} d ^ {2} \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}}\right) ^ {2} \log^ {2} T + \theta^ {3} d ^ {6} \log^ {3} T \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}}\right) ^ {3} + (1 - \alpha_ {t}) d \varepsilon_ {\text {J a c o b i}, t} (x)\right) \\ = 1 + \frac {d (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} + \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {t}) \left(\left\| \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) \left(x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0}\right) \mathrm {d} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} - \int_ {x _ {0}} p _ {X _ {0} \mid X _ {t}} (x _ {0} \mid x) \left\| x - \sqrt {\overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} x _ {0} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \mathrm {d} x _ {0}\right)}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}) (1 - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} \\ + O \left(\theta_ {t} (x) ^ {2} d ^ {2} \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}}\right) ^ {2} \log^ {2} T + \theta^ {3} d ^ {6} \log^ {3} T \left(\frac {1 - \alpha_ {t}}{\alpha_ {t} - \bar {\alpha} _ {t}}\right) ^ {3} + (1 - \alpha_ {t}) d \varepsilon_ {\text {J a c o b i}, t} (x)\right), \tag {141} \\ \end{array}
$$

with the proviso that

$$
\frac {d ^ {2} (1 - \alpha_ {t}) \log T}{\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t}} \leq \frac {8 c _ {1} d ^ {2} \log^ {2} T}{T} \leq c _ {1 2} \quad \mathrm {a n d} \quad (1 - \alpha_ {t}) d \varepsilon_ {\mathrm {J a c o b i}, t} (x) \leq \frac {c _ {1} d \varepsilon_ {\mathrm {J a c o b i} , t} (x) \log T}{T} \leq c _ {1 2}
$$

for some sufficiently small constant  $c_{12} > 0$  (see (39)).

# B.2 Proof of Lemma 5

In view of the definition (67), one has

$$
S _ {k} \left(y _ {T}\right) \leq c _ {1 4}, \quad \text {f o r a n y} k <   \tau \left(y _ {T}\right). \tag {142}
$$

Suppose instead that (69) does not hold true, namely,  $-\log q_k(y_k) > 2c_6d\log T$  for some  $k < \tau(y_T)$ , and we would like to show that this leads to contradiction.

Towards this, let  $1 < t \leq k$  be the smallest time step obeying

$$
\theta_ {t} \left(y _ {t}\right) = \max  \left\{- \frac {\log q _ {t} \left(y _ {t}\right)}{d \log T}, c _ {6} \right\} > 2 c _ {6} = 2 \theta_ {1} \left(y _ {1}\right), \tag {143}
$$

where the last identity holds since  $-\log q_{1}(y_{1}) \leq c_{6} d \log T$  and hence  $\theta_{1}(y_{1}) = \max \left\{-\frac{\log q_{1}(y_{1})}{d \log T}, c_{6}\right\} = c_{6}$ . We claim that  $t$  necessarily obeys

$$
2 c _ {6} <   \theta_ {t} (y _ {t}) \leq 4 c _ {6}. \tag {144}
$$

Assuming the validity of Claim (144) for the moment, it necessarily satisfies

$$
\theta_ {1} (y _ {1}), \theta_ {2} (y _ {2}), \dots , \theta_ {t} (y _ {t}) \in [ c _ {6}, 4 c _ {6} ].
$$

According to the relations (59) and (142), we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} c _ {6} = \theta_ {1} (y _ {1}) \leq \theta_ {t} (y _ {t}) - \theta_ {1} (y _ {1}) = - \frac {\log q _ {t} (y _ {t})}{d \log T} - \theta_ {1} (y _ {1}) \leq \frac {- \log q _ {t} (y _ {t}) + \log q _ {1} (y _ {1})}{d \log T} \\ = \frac {1}{d \log T} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {t - 1} \left(\log q _ {j} \left(y _ {j}\right) - \log q _ {j + 1} \left(y _ {j + 1}\right)\right) \\ \leq 2 c _ {1} + C _ {1 0} \left\{\frac {d \log^ {3} T}{T} + \frac {S _ {\tau (y _ {T}) - 1} (y _ {T})}{d \log T} \right\} <   3 c _ {1} \\ \end{array}
$$

under our sample size condition. This, however, cannot possibly hold if  $c_{6} \geq 3c_{1}$  as assumed for Lemma 5.

To finish up, it suffices to justify Claim (144). In order to see this, suppose instead that  $\theta_t(y_t) > 4c_6$ . Given relation (142) that  $S_k(y_T) \leq c_{14}$ , it can be readily seen from (57), (142) as well as the learning rate properties (39) that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \theta_ {t - 1} \left(y _ {t - 1}\right) = \theta_ {t} \left(y _ {t}\right) + \theta_ {t - 1} \left(y _ {t - 1}\right) - \theta_ {t} \left(y _ {t}\right) \\ = \theta_ {t} (y _ {t}) + \theta_ {t - 1} (y _ {t - 1}) + \frac {\log p _ {t} (y _ {t})}{d \log T} \geq \theta_ {t} (y _ {t}) - \frac {\log p _ {t - 1} (y _ {t - 1}) - \log p _ {t} (y _ {t})}{d \log T} \\ \geq \theta_ {t} (y _ {t}) - \frac {4 c _ {1} \left(5 \varepsilon_ {\mathrm {s c o r e} , t} (y _ {t}) \sqrt {\theta_ {t} (y _ {t}) d \log T} + 6 0 \theta_ {t} (y _ {t}) d \log T\right)}{d T} - \frac {\log 2}{d \log T} \\ \geq \theta_ {t} (y _ {t}) - \frac {4 c _ {1} \left(5 \varepsilon_ {\text {s c o r e} , t} (y _ {t}) \sqrt {d \log T} + 6 0 d \log T\right)}{d T} \theta_ {t} (y _ {t}) - \frac {\log 2}{d \log T} \\ > \frac {1}{2} \theta_ {t} (y _ {t}) > 2 c _ {6}, \\ \end{array}
$$

which is contradictory with the assumption that  $t$  is the smallest step obeying  $\theta_t(y_t) > 2c_6$ . Thus, we complete the proof of relation (69) as required.

# B.3 Proof of Lemma 6

Next, consider any  $y_{T}$ , with  $\{y_{T - 1},\dots ,y_1\}$  being the associated deterministic sequence (cf. (53))). As an immediate consequence of Lemma 5 and the definition (56) of  $\theta_t(\cdot)$ , one has

$$
\theta_ {t} \left(y _ {t}\right) \leq 2 c _ {6}, \quad \forall t <   \tau \left(y _ {T}\right) \tag {145}
$$

We then intend to invoke Lemma 4 to control the term of interest. To do so, note that Lemma 1, (39) and the definition (67) of  $\tau(y_{T})$  taken together reveal that: for all  $t < \tau(y_{T})$  one has

$$
\frac {d (1 - \alpha_ {t})}{2 (\alpha_ {t} - \overline {{\alpha}} _ {t})} \lesssim \frac {d \log T}{T} = o (1),
$$

$$
